483	### 5.5 Tombstone the rotated-out IPNS name (tombstone-and-keep)
484	
485	Approach (c) changes the k51 name and re-points parents, but `unenrollIpns` deletes **only** the schedule row (`republish.service.ts:257`) — the old `ipns_records` row persists and the publish gate has **zero revocation awareness**, so a revoked writer's cached key can publish to the old name **forever**, and resolve still serves it to stale links.
486	
487	On rotation, **tombstone** the old row (keep it, do **not** hard-delete):
488	
489	- the publish gate **rejects all writes** to a tombstoned name,
490	- resolve returns a tombstone / `410` (never stale content),
491	- the name is **TEE-unenrolled** — concretely, **removed from the republish batch** (today `unenrollIpns` only deletes the schedule row, which is *not* sufficient), so the lease-renewer (Section 6.4) is never handed the old name to re-extend. The renewal write is itself a publish, so the publish-gate tombstone check (Section 6.6) **must also reject the EOL-only renewal CAS** for a tombstoned name — otherwise a malicious relay that re-feeds the old signed record to an honest TEE could keep a revoked name's lease alive, defeating the "never stale content" promise.
492	
493	Tombstone-and-keep (rather than hard-delete) so stale links/bookmarks get an explicit "moved/revoked" signal rather than silent stale content.
494	
495	## 6. Resolve, republish, and the TEE signing contract
496	
497	This section is the resolve/republish/TEE model ratified in session 2 (decisions 14–20; the rotated-out-name tombstone, decision 21, lives in Section 5.5 since it is a write-revocation mechanic). It **supersedes the original "republisher is orthogonal" claim** (Section 4.6) and the interim "republisher sources canonical `latestCid`" patch: the fix is now achieved **structurally** (decisions 16–17) rather than by refreshing a snapshot. All claims below were verified against current code.
498	
499	### 6.1 Resolve precedence: `generation` is the anti-rollback authority; resolve-source is a latency layer
500	
501	The IPFS/IPNS network is permissionless — its only anti-rollback is **"higher sequence wins; on equal sequence, later EOL wins"** (verified against boxo/go-ipns `compare`). So the network **cannot be the integrity authority**; `generation` (M1, Section 4.3) is. Resolve-source (network vs DB) is a **latency / availability layer beneath** that authority.
502	
503	Near-term the **DB is canonical**: the relay writes the DB **synchronously before** the fire-and-forget someguy push (`ipns.service.ts:106-144`), so the DB **leads** the DHT by ~10–30 s. The intuition that "the network is fresher" is **inverted** in this relay-mediated topology. "Network strictly ahead of DB" is therefore an **alarm**, not a normal branch.
504	
505	The maintainer's "network as the single source of truth, DB as fallback" ideal is reachable for **confidentiality** once M1 ships (generation rejects any cross-generation rollback regardless of source), **but it stays gated on the within-generation floor (Section 6.5) — M1 alone does not unlock it.** A re-pointed network-first resolve therefore remains a post-M1 **v2 move**, not something this design enables; near-term, DB-canonical with M1 + the seq-floor is the resolve posture.
506	
507	### 6.2 Sequence advances iff the CID changes; republish never increments
508	
509	**A republish re-signs the *same* sequence with a fresh EOL.** IPNS record selection's equal-sequence→later-EOL tiebreak lets the refreshed record win without consuming a sequence. The relay publish path already does this on the idempotent branch (`ipns.service.ts:306-317`, "D-09"); but the **TEE 6-hour republisher still does `+ 1n`** (`apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts:79`) and must be unified to the no-increment path.
510	
511	Incrementing on republish is not just unnecessary, it is **harmful** — it races client writes for sequence numbers and widens the replay window. This invariant **alone** closes the Section 4.6 republisher-stale-CID rollback: a re-signed stale CID stays at its old sequence and is dominated by any genuine forward client publish. Increment policy moves **out of the enclave into the relay**.
512	
513	### 6.3 Collapse the dual-source record state
514	
515	`ipns_republish_schedule` duplicates `latestCid` / `sequenceNumber` / `encryptedIpnsKey` / `keyEpoch` (`republish-schedule.entity.ts:39-60`) and the TEE signs **that** snapshot (`republish.service.ts:101-102`), which goes stale on a normal content write (refreshed only on key-enrollment).
516	
517	Make the canonical **`ipns_records` row the sole source** of the TEE's signing inputs; reduce the schedule to scheduling metadata (`next_republish_at`, `consecutive_failures`, `status`) — or fold those columns into `ipns_records` and drop the table. This structurally kills both the stale-CID rollback **and** a latent availability bug: today the republisher keeps the *old* CID's network record fresh while the canonical *new* CID's record expires ~48 h after the client's one-time publish (masked only by DB-canonical resolve).
518	
519	### 6.4 The TEE is a record-lease-renewer, not a signer of supplied scalars
520	
521	Clients self-sign every content change with their client-held Ed25519 key (`packages/core/src/ipns/create-record.ts`; the relay only verifies, `ipns.service.ts:100`), so the TEE never needs to originate a CID. New enclave contract:
522	
523	- The relay sends the **marshaled existing `signedRecord`**.
524	- The TEE **parses it, verifies its signature**, and re-emits a record with the **same value (CID) and same sequence**, only a **later EOL**.
525	- The TEE therefore **cannot originate or repoint a CID** — it can only extend a lease.
526	
527	"Verify against what the network resolves" was **rejected** as the mechanism: it is circular (the relay controls the enclave's network view), inverts the ratified source-of-truth (the network is the lagging untrusted replica), and fights the propagation window. Worst residual on an **honest** enclave: a malicious relay replays an *old* lower-seq validly-signed record for renewal — dominated by sequence and caught by M1.
528	
529	### 6.5 Complete the resolve anti-rollback (the seq-floor companion to M1)
530	
531	`generation` only bumps on rotation, so **within-generation** version rollback — serving an old, genuinely-signed, lower-seq record in the *same* generation — passes every current check. Add:
532	
533	1. **Durable per-node `{nodeId → highestSeq}` high-water** on the client (the sequence analog of the M1 generation map), rejecting `seq < high-water` regardless of resolve source.
534	2. **Bind a version floor** into the `SealedChildRef` at (re)share (the `versionFloor` field, Section 2.6), so first-contact and cold/reset devices inherit an **owner-vouched floor** from the parent chain (the `SealedChildRef` mirrors generation but not version today). The operative form is a **seq integer** driving the `seq ≥ versionFloor` check; a head-Node hash is an alternative that pins the *exact* first-contact head (rejecting all forward versions until the durable seq high-water takes over), so do not use the hash form as a standing floor — once the high-water of item 1 is established it supersedes the share-time floor.
535	3. The relay must **never silently fall through to an ungated network record.** When the canonical DB row is unparseable (`parseCachedRecord` null), the response is **case-dependent** — do not leave it as an undifferentiated "fail closed *or* floor":
536	   - **Expected null `signedRecord` (shared-folder rows).** This is the *normal* state for shared-folder rows (`signedRecord`/`public_key` legitimately null, see Section 7.1), not corruption — failing closed here would break legitimate shared-folder resolve. Apply the `seq ≥ storedSeq` floor from the DB `sequenceNumber` column to the network record.
537	   - **`signedRecord`-CID ≠ `latestCid` mismatch.** This is corruption or an attack (a row whose signed bytes disagree with the canonical CID). **Fail closed** — never serve it and never fall through.
538	
539	This closes the Section 4.3-M1 colluding-relay-drops-publish residual — the durable client floor is the signed-signal-independent defense.
540	
541	### 6.6 Atomic publish CAS
542	
543	`publishRecord` is a non-atomic `findOne → gate → save` with no row lock / `@VersionColumn` / conditional UPDATE, so two concurrent forward writers both at `dbSeq = N` both pass the gate and the second `save` clobbers the first — a `200`'d write silently lost (generation cannot help; same generation). Decision 16's single canonical row makes it the sole serialization point, and the lease-renewal of Section 6.4 hits the idempotent branch on it, **widening** the race.
544	
545	Fix: a single compare-and-set —
546	
547	```sql
548	UPDATE ipns_records SET … WHERE ipnsName = :n AND sequenceNumber = :expected
549	```
550	
551	— 0 rows affected ⇒ 409. The idempotent / renewal write is guarded identically (`WHERE sequenceNumber = :loaded`) so an EOL-only renewal can **never** regress `latestCid` / `sequenceNumber` from a stale in-memory row.
552	
553	### 6.7 Three enclave bindings beyond the lease-renewer contract
554	
555	The relay still feeds the enclave the epoch scalars, the wrapped key, and the claimed name. Harden:
556	
557	1. **Internal epoch derivation.** The TEE derives `currentEpoch` / `previousEpoch` from its **own clock + epoch schedule** (never the relay's scalars), with re-wrap targets restricted to an **enclave-enumerated set** — else a malicious relay coerces re-wrapping every IPNS key under an attacker-chosen epoch pubkey for later offline forgery.
558	2. **Name↔key binding.** Before emitting, assert `publicKeyFromIpnsName(ipnsName) == pubkey(decryptedKey) == record.pubkey` (closes batch cross-contamination / key-confusion / cross-name forgery).
559	3. **Migration durability.** Because a malicious relay can drop the returned `upgradedEncryptedKey` and brick a name at epoch retirement, make the **client** the recovery path (periodic re-enroll / re-wrap from its held key), or have the TEE **refuse to renew a key older than `currentEpoch − 1`**.
560	
561	### 6.8 Accepted residuals (resolve / republish / TEE)
562	
563	- **Compromised enclave / leaked epoch key = total loss** — every wrapped IPNS key is unwrappable offline and every vault repointable. The lease-renewer contract (Section 6.4) bounds the *honest* enclave's worst case to lower-seq replay; it does **not** contain a malicious enclave. This rests entirely on **Phala remote-attestation** (enforced on every epoch-key provisioning) + **epoch-rotation cadence** (bounds the exposure window). Stated as the explicit systemic residual.
564	- **Equal-sequence EOL selection** is a freshness/availability nuisance only: under decisions 6.2 + 6.4 same-sequence records must share a CID, so the relay's choice of which equal-seq record a client sees cannot fork content. Escalate only if equal-seq distinct-CID records can ever be minted.
565	- **Already-distributed ciphertext stays readable** (ADR 0002) — unchanged.
566	
567	## 7. Blast radius, cutover ordering, and test strategy
568	
569	### 7.1 Blast radius (most → least invasive)
570	
571	| Layer | Change | Invasiveness |
572	|---|---|---|
573	| `packages/core` | Replace `FolderMetadata`/`FileMetadata`/`FilePointer`/`FolderEntry` + vault `encryptedRootFolderKey` with `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` + codecs (two sealed bodies, content self-seal, structured write chain) | Highest (keystone) |
574	| `crates/fuse` | Symmetric child-key unwrap; delete `spawn_file_meta_reencrypt`; add `rotateReadFromNode`; unify scope-exit; **grant-root awareness in `delete`/`rename`/`move`**; durable M1 generation + seq high-water; `Node` as Rust enum | High (Rust, two clients) |
575	| `apps/tee-worker` + `packages/core/src/ipns` | **TEE enclave contract rewrite** (lease-renewer: receive marshaled record, verify signature, extend EOL; internal epoch derivation; name↔key binding); republish no longer increments | High |
576	| `packages/sdk` + `sdk-core` | Read-chain navigation; rewrite `shared-write.ts` (structured write-body, role `0x04`); delete `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`; rotation driver; `bin/*` re-link; invite claim re-wrap | High |
577	| `apps/web` | Replace `executeLazyRotation` with `rotateReadFromNode`; drop per-mutation fan-out; reconcile `folderTree`; durable M1 generation + seq high-water | Medium-High |
578	| `apps/api` | Delete `share_keys`; slim `shares` (`readDescriptorRef`/`writeDescriptorRef`); rotation bookkeeping; **collapse `ipns_republish_schedule` duplicated columns into `ipns_records`**; **atomic conditional-UPDATE publish CAS**; **tombstone state + publish-gate rejection + resolve tombstone/410 + TEE unenroll**; client-side re-enroll/re-wrap recovery path; `resolveRecord` fail-closed fall-through; **rename `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`** (entity `IpnsRecord`, repository) and **drop `folder_ipns.public_key`** | Medium-High |
579	| `packages/crypto` | Add `sealAesGcmAad`/`unsealAesGcmAad` + `buildNodeAad` (TS + Rust twin + KAT; frozen byte encoding incl. role `0x04`) | Low (additive) |
580	
581	`folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`: the table holds the IPNS records for files, root, bin, and the vault-key blob too, not just folders — rename it (free under greenfield). Drop `folder_ipns.public_key`: it is the raw 32-byte Ed25519 IPNS pubkey (`ipns.service.ts:72-79` validates length 32 and `deriveIpnsName(pubkey) === ipnsName`), not the user's secp256k1 `publicKey` (the owner is tracked by `userId`); it is null for shared-folder rows and derivable from the k51 name via `publicKeyFromIpnsName`, so drop the nullable column and always recover from the name (removes the null-row footgun behind two Phase-60 regressions).
582	
583	### 7.2 Buildable cutover order
584	
585	1. `packages/crypto` — `sealAesGcmAad`/`unsealAesGcmAad` + `buildNodeAad` (TS) + byte-identical Rust twin + a committed cross-language KAT fixture (frozen byte encoding, role `0x04`) asserted by both. Self-contained, no consumers break.
586	2. `packages/core` — `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` + codecs, two sealed bodies, content self-seal, structured write chain, `versionFloor`. Keystone — nothing below typechecks until done.
587	3. `packages/sdk-core` — read-chain navigation + `rotateReadFromNode` driver (in named files, not a fat `index.ts` barrel — coverage excludes barrels). Rebuild dist before consumers.
588	4. `packages/sdk` — `shared-write.ts` rewrite (structured write-body, (c) full rotation); delete `addShareKeys`/`reWrapForRecipients`; `bin/*` re-link; invite claim re-wrap.
589	5. `apps/api` — delete `share_keys`; slim `shares`; rename `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records` + drop `public_key`; collapse the schedule's duplicated columns; atomic publish CAS; tombstone state + publish-gate rejection + resolve fail-closed fall-through. Run `pnpm api:generate`, commit the regenerated client (pre-commit `check-api-client.sh`).
590	6. `apps/tee-worker` — lease-renewer contract (verify marshaled record, extend EOL, no increment); internal epoch derivation; name↔key binding. Round-trip the TEE/republish E2E.
591	7. `apps/web` — `executeLazyRotation` → `rotateReadFromNode`; drop per-mutation fan-out; reconcile `folderTree` against `sequenceNumber` before publishes; durable M1 generation + seq high-water.
592	8. `crates/fuse` — symmetric unwrap; delete reencrypt; unify scope-exit; grant-root awareness; durable client floors; strict-verify each republish; `Node` enum. Budget a Windows CI round-trip for winfsp (`windows/*` can't compile on macOS; `Cargo Check & Test (Windows)` is authoritative; watch the `super::` vs `super::super::` nesting trap, and the `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:1182` reencrypt twin).
593	
594	Strict-verify caveat: recover the Ed25519 pubkey from the k51 name via `publicKeyFromIpnsName`, **never** from the (now-dropped) `public_key` column. Each rotation republish must round-trip the verified chokepoint.
595	
596	### 7.3 Test strategy (must-pass-before-merge first)
597	
598	1. **Rotation crash-safety / resume (the suite that must exist before merge).** Deterministic fault-injection that aborts the walk after each node and asserts: (i) the revoked recipient can't unwrap from root after the root step; (ii) re-run converges; (iii) no incorrect double-bump. Extend `tests/sdk-e2e` (the only real client→API IPNS publish/resolve round-trip) with abort-and-resume cases.
599	2. **CRIT-1 content-key rotation.** Rotate a file node, publish a new version, assert a holder of the **old** `readKey`/`fileKey` cannot decrypt the new version.
600	3. **HIGH-3 multi-rooted re-mint.** Independently-shared single file inside a deleted/moved subtree — assert the inner grantee's `readDescriptorRef` is re-minted (not orphaned), and a revoked recipient is cut.
601	4. **HIGH-4 add-during-rotation.** Concurrent upload during rotation — assert the new child is not dropped (re-merge on 409).
602	5. **M1 generation downgrade.** Relay serves a stale signed record post-rotation — assert the client fails closed on generation regression (durable high-water survives restart).
603	6. **AAD transplant resistance.** Replay a valid `readKeySealed` under a different `childId`/`role`/`generation` — assert `unsealAesGcmAad` rejects.
604	7. **TS↔Rust AAD KAT.** One committed fixture asserted by both `packages/crypto/__tests__` and a Rust `#[test]` — a byte mismatch is silent total decryption failure.
605	8. **CTR content + version.** A CTR content and a CTR `VersionEntry` both decrypt under the v3 content schema.
606	9. **Scope-exit only.** A private delete/move (no covering grant) performs **zero** rotations (pure relink); a shared delete rotates + revokes.
607	10. **Bin restore.** Restore is a pure re-link (no re-encrypt).
608	11. **Invite claim.** Claim re-wraps the share-root `readKey` to the claimer; revoke (rotate) cuts the link and all claimers at once.
609	12. **Republisher stale-CID.** Republisher re-signs mid-rotation → assert the revoked CID is never re-signed and never served; assert republish does **not** increment the sequence.
610	13. **Within-generation rollback.** Relay serves an old lower-seq same-generation signed record → client rejects via the seq high-water.
611	14. **First-contact / cold-device rollback.** Fresh client with no local high-water → the `SealedChildRef` `versionFloor` rejects a below-floor seq.
612	15. **`parseCachedRecord`-null fall-through.** An unparseable canonical DB row → resolve fails closed (or applies the seq floor), never serving an ungated network record.
613	16. **Concurrent forward publishes.** Two devices at the same `dbSeq` → exactly one 409, zero lost updates.
614	17. **Lease-renewal racing a forward publish.** The renewal never regresses `latestCid`/`sequenceNumber`.
615	18. **TEE name↔key binding.** A swapped wrapped key / wrong-name slot → the enclave refuses to emit.
616	19. **TEE epoch self-derivation.** An attacker-supplied `currentEpoch` is ignored; re-wrap only targets an enclave-valid epoch.
617	20. **Tombstoned name.** Writes to a tombstoned name are rejected; resolve returns the tombstone, not stale content.
618	21. **winfsp read-path.** `gh workflow run "Cargo Check & Test (Windows)"` is authoritative.
619	
620	Keep checker subagents to static analysis only (no concurrent vitest — RAM starvation).
621	
622	## 8. Forward-looking capability-layer fit (Tier 3 — not required)
623	
624	Bottom line: the Tier-1 core is cleanly extensible to the speculative agent-capability ideas (TTL, per-file/read-only scope, op-count caps), the extension point is the write/grant plane and never the read chain, and building any of it now would be premature. The deliverable stands on Tier 1+2 alone.
625	
626	What is already delivered by Tier 1 (not future work): **per-file and read-only scope.** Single-file shares fall out of content self-sealing (Section 2.9); read-only-vs-write is the `permission` column plus the omitted write body. Nothing to add later.
627	
628	What is sound to extend later, for free: the grant row is already `O(recipients)` and column-extensible — adding `ttl`/`opCap`/`capabilityId` columns is a non-breaking migration; `generation` already gives per-node revocation granularity.
629	
630	What is unsound and must not be built as described: **read-side TTL/op-caps are cryptographically unenforceable.** Once a reader holds key + CID, IPFS serves the content forever; a "read expires in 1h" claim is security theater unless you re-encrypt + rotate at expiry, which is just scheduled read-revocation (the Section 4 machinery), not a cheap TTL. Time-boxing and op-caps are meaningful **only on the write path**, and only if a mediated mechanism is ever chosen.
631	
632	The two cheap, non-committal hooks already in this design: the write body is sealed and separable (the forward-compat surface), and revocation routes through `rotateReadFromNode` keyed on `generation`. Do **not** add `ttl`/`opCap`/`capabilityId` to `Node` or `SealedChildRef`, and do not name an `authzTokenId` as the hook (that pre-decides mediated writes). If a capability layer is ever built, it attaches to the write/grant plane, is unenforceable on reads, and needs nothing in the node schema. If Tier 3 is never pursued, nothing here is wasted.
633	
634	## 9. Decisions resolved and remaining open questions
635	
636	### 9.1 Resolved by the grilling sessions (ADRs + decisions)
637	
638	- **Write mechanism** → **(c) full Ed25519 rotation**, ratified (ADR 0001). (Full (a) mediated remains the documented runner-up with explicit flip conditions, Section 5.4.)
639	- **Terminology** → `readKey` / `writeKey` adopted; `folderKey` / `fileKey` / `rootFolderKey` retired; `shares` row uses `readDescriptorRef` / `writeDescriptorRef`; `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`; `public_key` column dropped (CONTEXT.md).
640	- **Move-within-scope cost** → FUSE gains grant-root awareness and computes **exact per-grant scope** (Section 3.5); benign within-scope moves do not over-rotate.
641	- **Migration** → none; greenfield, `node/v3` is the sole codec (Section 1.6).
642	- **Republisher / resolve / TEE** → DB-canonical resolve with M1 + seq-floor authority; republish never increments; TEE is a lease-renewer; atomic publish CAS; tombstone rotated-out names (Section 6).
643	
644	### 9.2 Remaining open questions
645	
646	1. **Co-writer offline handling.** Under (c), a co-writer offline during a write-key rotation cannot write until they re-fetch the re-wrapped key (Section 5.3). Accepted as explicit, or is a grace/notification mechanism wanted?
647	2. **Rotation host.** Eager million-node rotation is owner-online and resumable; desktop (FUSE) is the natural host. Is it acceptable that a pure-web user without the desktop app pays a long, chunked, multi-session rotation for a large revoke?
648	3. **Write-recipient deletions vs owner-held sub-shares.** When a write-recipient (C) deletes, moves out, or overwrites a node inside a shared folder that the OWNER had *independently* sub-shared to a third party (e.g. a single file shared to D), C can unlink it immediately (C holds the folder's write keys and signs the folder publish) but **cannot** cryptographically revoke D's grant — only the owner holds that node's rotation keys and authority over the `shares` rows. So the unlink and the revocation are split across two principals. Options: (a) leave the sub-share dangling until the owner's next sync runs a reconciliation/rotation pass — bounded exposure window in which D retains read access to the now-binned snapshot (already irreducibly readable via IPFS, ADR 0002, so arguably acceptable); (b) block a write-recipient from destroying a node that carries owner-owned sub-shares — but that requires the relay to tell the writer "this node has active grants," leaking share existence to a delegate; (c) have C's mutation enqueue an owner-signed revocation request that the owner (or the owner's desktop/TEE-mediated agent) executes on next online. Decide the authority model and the acceptable exposure window. (Surfaced from the FS-permutations walkthrough — flow `write-recipient-c-delete-file1`.)
649	
```

10	
11	# SEED-001: utilize the phala api to programatically turn the TEE on when a refresh is necessary, and turn it off when no TEE workload is present, with the aim of reducing phala costs
12	
13	## Why This Matters
14	
15	The production TEE worker does exactly one job: signing IPNS records on the
16	republish cron. It is otherwise a persistent Express server with no internal
17	scheduler, woken only when the API's BullMQ cron POSTs `/republish`.
18	
19	- Cadence: fixed cron `0 */6 * * *` → 4 runs/day (00:00, 06:00, 12:00, 18:00 UTC).
20	- Per-run duration: up to 2000 due entries → up to 20 sequential TEE batches of 100. Healthy run is well under a minute; pathological fully-degraded run caps ~10 min.
21	- Freshness deadline: records are signed with a 48h EOL and republished every 6h → 8× safety margin. The hard requirement is that each record be re-signed within 48h or resolvers treat it as expired.
22	
23	Duty cycle: ~4–10 active minutes/day out of 1440 (**<1%**). The CVM pays always-on
24	rates to do under 1% work. The economic motivation is real, but see the cost
25	reality check below — the absolute prize is likely single-digit dollars/month on
26	one staging-sized CVM.
27	
28	## Feasibility Findings (from 2026-06-16 understand pass)
29	
30	### Key continuity — the make-or-break — is favorable
31	
32	The worker persists **no** private key; keys are deterministically derived per
33	epoch via dstack `getKey('cipherbox/ipns-republish', epoch-${N})`, bound to the
34	CVM **app_id**.
35	
36	- `stop`/`start` preserves app_id → identical `teePublicKey` re-derives. This is the safe primitive.
37	- The landmine is **destroy + recreate into a new app_id** → every epoch's key changes → all previously ECIES-wrapped IPNS keys become permanently undecryptable. Documented as a CRITICAL rule in `apps/tee-worker/docker-compose.phala.yml` and `.planning/phases/35-phala-testnet-tee-migration/35-RESEARCH.md:185-187`.
38	- There is **zero attestation/quote verification anywhere in the repo** (grep for `getQuote|attestation|dcap|RA-TLS` → no hits). Clients trust the `teePublicKey` the API serves, so quote-instability across boots is a non-issue today. It would become a blocker only if attestation is ever added.
39	
40	### Cold-start is irrelevant to the deadline
41	
42	Even a 20–25 min cold-start (large image, decompression-bound) sits inside the
43	48h freshness window with ~47h slack and ~5.5h before the next cron. Latency only
44	matters because it lengthens the billable active window and adds orchestration
45	(start → poll `/health` ready → POST `/republish` → stop).
46	
47	### Cost model
48	
49	- Phala bills per-second, no monthly minimum: small $0.06/hr (~$43/mo), medium $0.12/hr (~$86/mo), large $0.23/hr.
50	- A STOPPED CVM stops compute charges immediately but **still bills storage** (~$0.10/GB/mo); only DELETE halts storage too.
51	- GPU TEE has a 24h minimum re-applied each launch → cycling only pays off for CPU/TDX CVMs. Our worker is CPU/TDX, so we are in the favorable class.
52	- Net ~85–95% compute reduction, bounded below by storage and cold-start duration. Absolute dollar figure unconfirmed (CVM size/disk/image tag not pinned in repo).
53	
54	## Architecture Options
55	
56	- **A — Stop/start the same CVM (recommended primitive).** Orchestrator runs `phala cvms start`, polls `/health` until ready, runs batches, then `phala cvms stop`. Preserves app_id → keys safe. Lowest key-continuity risk, modest savings. Still pays storage; needs the API (or a thin scheduler) to own start/stop + readiness gating + the cron→start coupling.
57	- **B — Destroy/recreate with same app identity.** Removes storage cost too, but high risk: this is the exact path the bricked-keys warning is about. Reject unless storage cost is shown to be material.
58	- **C — Keep always-on but downsize.** If prod is medium/large, drop to small. Zero new orchestration, zero key risk, captures a chunk of the savings. Strong "do this first" baseline.
59	- **D — Serverless / scale-to-zero TEE.** Phala has no first-party scale-to-zero or cron CVM feature — any cycling is custom orchestration we build and maintain. A failed `start` becomes a missed republish. Not justified at current scale.
60	
61	## Recommendation
62	
63	Sequence by risk-adjusted value:
64	
65	1. Downsize the always-on CVM to `small` if it isn't already (Option C) — most of the dollars, zero risk, zero new infra.
66	2. Two ~30-min derisking spikes before any build: (a) on a throwaway TDX CVM, derive `getKey()` → `stop`→`start` → re-derive, assert identical `teePublicKey`; (b) measure real `phala cvms start`→`/health`-ready time and whether `start` re-pulls.
67	3. If both spikes pass → Option A (stop/start) behind the existing DB-backoff safety net.
68	4. Reject B and D at current scale.
69	
70	## When to Surface
71	
72	Trigger: when relevant. Surfaces during `/gsd-new-milestone` when the milestone
73	scope matches TEE / republishing / infra-cost work.
74	
75	## Scope Estimate
76	
77	Small–medium. Option C is a config change (near-zero). The spikes are ~30 min
78	each. Option A is a bounded feature: a start→poll-health→republish→stop sequence
79	in the API/scheduler, Phala API credentials wired into the service (none exist in
80	CI today), and explicit failure handling. The DB backoff state machine already
81	degrades a missed wake gracefully within the 48h window, which caps the blast
82	radius.
83	
84	## Open Questions / Spikes Before Committing
85	
86	1. **Key-continuity spike (blocking):** derive `getKey()` for an epoch, `stop`→`start`, re-derive, assert identical `teePublicKey`. The one true make-or-break check.
87	2. **Cold-start measurement:** time `phala cvms start` → `/health` ready for our actual image; note whether `start` re-pulls.
88	3. **Disk/storage footprint + CVM size:** `phala cvms get` for prod → size, disk GB, image tag. Turns the cost model from estimate into a number.
89	4. **Prod-vs-staging CVM identity:** the prod compose is named `cipherbox-tee-staging` with `CIPHERBOX_ENVIRONMENT=staging`. Confirm whether a distinct production CVM exists or this staging-named one IS production — don't cycle the wrong one.
90	5. **Orchestration ownership + failure mode:** decide who calls start/stop (API service vs external scheduler) and make the missed-wake behavior explicit (DB backoff: retrying → stale after 10 fails, within the 48h margin).
91	6. **Active-epoch delivery:** `TEE_CURRENT_EPOCH` is required per the worker README but set in neither compose file — how the active epoch reaches the production CVM is not captured in the repo. Resolve before any lifecycle automation.
92	
93	## Breadcrumbs
94	
95	- `apps/tee-worker/src/services/tee-keys.ts` — per-epoch deterministic key derivation (HKDF seed in simulator, dstack `getKey()` bound to app_id in CVM mode).
96	- `apps/tee-worker/docker-compose.phala.yml` — production CVM config; inline CRITICAL rule against delete-and-recreate; manual `phala deploy` provisioning.
97	- `apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts`, `apps/tee-worker/src/services/ipns-signer.ts` — in-enclave decrypt/sign, 48h record lifetime.
98	- `apps/api/src/republish/` — BullMQ `0 */6 * * *` cron, batch-of-100 dispatch, DB backoff state machine (retrying → stale after 10 fails).
99	- `apps/api/src/tee/tee.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/tee/tee-key-state.service.ts` — API↔worker HTTP boundary (`TEE_WORKER_URL` + bearer `TEE_WORKER_SECRET`), `teePublicKey`/keyEpoch persistence and 4-week grace rotation.
100	- `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:145-149`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:225-256` — TEE/Phala deployment topology.
101	- `CLAUDE.md` — "TEE Republishing: TEE worker republishes IPNS every 6 hours — Phala Cloud CVM in production"; "Key Epochs: TEE public keys rotate with 4-week grace period".
102	
103	## Notes
104	
105	Captured via one-shot seed capture; enriched 2026-06-16 from a four-agent
106	understand pass (republish subsystem, TEE key lifecycle, deployment topology,
107	Phala lifecycle API). No architecture changed — the brief lives here until a
108	TEE/infra-cost milestone surfaces it.
109	
110	Project memory: `project-phala-credits-expected` — staging TEE may return to Phala
111	Cloud; the inert `PHALA_*` GitHub staging env entries are intentional, not
112	orphaned.
113

1	# CipherBox
2	
3	A zero-knowledge encrypted IPFS/IPNS vault. The server is an untrusted relay that never sees plaintext or any unwrapped key. This glossary pins the vocabulary for the metadata, key-chaining, and sharing model (the `node/v3` design). It complements — does not replace — the terminology table in `CLAUDE.md`.
4	
5	## Keys
6	
7	**readKey**:
8	A per-node 32-byte AES key that seals a node's read-body (its children and, for a file, its content). Chained parent-to-child: a parent seals each child's `readKey` under its own. Holding a node's `readKey` unwraps its whole subtree with symmetric AES.
9	_Avoid_: folderKey, fileKey, rootFolderKey, nodeKey
10	
11	**writeKey**:
12	A per-node 32-byte AES key that seals a node's write-body (the Ed25519 signing material). A read grant never conveys it, so a read-only holder can never reach a signing key.
13	_Avoid_: (none)
14	
15	## Counters
16	
17	Three distinct clocks; never conflate them.
18	
19	**generation**:
20	A per-node `u32` that bumps only on a read-key rotation (revocation / scope exit). It is the AAD epoch and the rotation convergence witness; authoritative only on the child's own published envelope.
21	_Avoid_: epoch, version (for this concept)
22	
23	**keyEpoch**:
24	The TEE public-key rotation counter (write-plane). Unrelated to read-key rotation.
25	_Avoid_: generation, bare "epoch"
26	
27	**sequenceNumber**:
28	The IPNS record counter that bumps on every publish; enforced forward-only by the relay's CAS gate.
29	_Avoid_: generation, "seq" in prose
30	
31	## Metadata model
32	
33	**Node**:
34	The single metadata object for a folder, file, or vault root, discriminated by `kind`. Carries an independently sealed read-body and write-body.
35	_Avoid_: FolderMetadata, FileMetadata, FilePointer, FolderEntry (all v2-legacy, retired)
36	
37	**SealedChildRef**:
38	A parent's link to one child, living inside the parent's read-body: the child's name, `ipnsName`, `generation` mirror, and the child's `readKey` sealed under the parent's `readKey`.
39	_Avoid_: FolderChild, FolderEntry
40	
41	**ipnsRecord**:
42	The signed IPNS data structure (name to CID, sequence, signature) and the row that stores it.
43	_Avoid_: "IPNS entry"; the table is `ipns_records`, not `folder_ipns`
44	
45	## Sharing
46	
47	**grant**:
48	One row in the `shares` table conveying read or write access to a share-root node — the wrapped key(s) for a single recipient. The only data-plane DB residue of a share.
49	_Avoid_: ShareGrant (type name), share_keys (the deleted per-item table)
50	
51	**readDescriptorRef / writeDescriptorRef**:
52	The ECIES-wrapped `readKey` / `writeKey` on a grant row, sealed to the recipient's secp256k1 public key.
53	_Avoid_: readKeyEcies, encryptedKey
54	
55	**scope exit**:
56	The condition where a node leaves the reachable scope of an active grantee. The only trigger for a read-key rotation; a node with no covering grant rotates never.
57	_Avoid_: (none)
58	
59	**bin**:
60	The owner's recoverable-delete space. A deleted node is re-linked into it as a `BinEntry` sealed under the bin's own `readKey`, rather than purged.
61	_Avoid_: trash, recycle bin (in code identifiers)
62	
63	**invite**:
64	A claimable share to an as-yet-unknown recipient: the share-root `readKey` wrapped to an ephemeral key whose private half travels in the link, re-wrapped to the claimer's key on claim.
65	_Avoid_: "link share" (in code identifiers — use invite)
66	
```

**Milestone v2.0: Metadata and Sharing Refactor**

**Goal:** Replace the DB-driven `share_keys` sharing model with metadata-driven read key-chaining (`node/v3`), and close the two confirmed revocation gaps (lazy/unsound read-revoke; un-rotatable write delegation).

**Source of truth:** `.planning/design/2026-06-26-sharing-read-keychaining-design.md` + ADR 0001 (write-revocation = full Ed25519 rotation) + ADR 0002 (read-revoke protects future content only) + `CONTEXT.md` glossary. Greenfield — no migration, `node/v3` is the sole codec.

**Tiered scope from the design:**
- **Tier 1 (firm):** unified `Node` schema, two sealed bodies, content self-seal, AAD-bound crypto primitive, read chain, resumable rotation engine (CRIT-1 content-key rotation, M1 generation defense, HIGH-3 multi-rooted re-mint, HIGH-4 add-during-rotation), unified scope-exit rule, bin + invites.
- **Tier 2 (ratified):** write-revocation via (c) full Ed25519 rotation + tombstone; resolve/republish/TEE contract rewrite (DB-canonical + generation authority, seq-floor, TEE lease-renewer, atomic publish CAS).
- **Tier 3:** explicitly NOT built (capability-layer fit is a forward-compat note only).

**Blast radius:** all 8 layers — `packages/crypto` → `core` → `sdk-core` → `sdk` → `apps/api` → `apps/tee-worker` → `apps/web` → `crates/fuse` (incl. a Windows/winfsp CI round-trip). 21-item must-pass-before-merge test strategy defined in §7.3.

**Key context:** v2.0 is a major refactor (replaces v1.0/v1.1 read paths outright). Three open questions remain in §9.2 (co-writer offline handling, rotation host for pure-web users, write-recipient-vs-owner-sub-share authority).

Before I write PROJECT.md and define requirements, three upfront decisions:

Decisions to log? → Add to Key Decisions\n5. \"What This Is\" still accurate? → Update if drifted\n\n**After each milestone** (via `/gsd-complete-milestone`):\n\n1. Full review of all sections\n2. Core Value check — still the right priority?\n3. Audit Out of Scope — reasons still valid?\n4. Update Context with current state\n\n---\n\nLast updated: 2026-06-27 — Milestone 4 (v2.0 Metadata and Sharing Refactor) started. Scope locked to Tier 1 (read key-chaining + resumable read-rotation) + Tier 2 (write-revocation via ADR 0001 full Ed25519 rotation + resolve/republish/TEE contract rewrite). Tier 3 capability layer and SEED-001 TEE cost-cycling deferred. Greenfield `node/v3` sole codec. Requirements in `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md`; design source of truth in `.planning/design/2026-06-26-sharing-read-keychaining-design.md` + ADR 0001/0002 + `CONTEXT.md`. v1.1 carried items (Phase 39 D-02/D-06, HARD-11 staging smoke-test) remain open."
}

# Architecture: IPFS Infrastructure v1.1

**Domain:** IPNS reliability, database minimization, BYO-IPFS, performance baselines
**Researched:** 2026-03-07
**Confidence:** HIGH (existing codebase analyzed; Kubo APIs verified against official docs)

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Existing Architecture Summary](#1-existing-architecture-summary)
2. [IPNS Resolution: Replace delegated-ipfs.dev](#2-ipns-resolution-replace-delegated-ipfsdev)
3. [Database Minimization: Migration Path](#3-database-minimization-migration-path)
4. [BYO-IPFS Node Support](#4-byo-ipfs-node-support)
5. [Performance Instrumentation](#5-performance-instrumentation)
=== questions.md head ===
# Research Questions

Open questions captured for deeper investigation. Each should be resolved (with
evidence) before the dependent decision is committed.

## Agent-native milestone — validation gate (2026-06-22)

Source: exploration session — see `notes/next-milestone-agent-native-zk-storage.md`.
These gate committing the agent-native milestone to the ROADMAP. Build the no-regret
moves first; require credible answers to these before the full pivot or the
"stop competing with Drive" messaging.

- [ ] **Payload / moat:** Which payload makes zero-knowledge *load-bearing* rather than
  nice-to-have — sensitive user PII under a revocable grant (strongest), the agent's own
  memory/RAG (weakest, commodity vs web3 storage), or agent-to-agent confidential
  exchange (novel)? The payload decides the moat and the customer.
- [ ] **First customer / design-partner LOI:** Is there an identifiable first paying
  agent-consumer, and will they pay for ZK *specifically* (vs defaulting to
  Storacha/Lighthouse/Walrus on cost + ecosystem)? Get one LOI before committing.
- [ ] **Moat depth:** ZK + revocable delegation is ~one feature deep; funded incumbents
```

## RESEARCH COMPLETE

**Project:** v2.0 Metadata and Sharing Refactor (node/v3)
**Mode:** Feasibility/Pitfalls — brownfield implementation risk surface
**Confidence:** HIGH

### Key Findings

- **Silent total decryption failure** is the top risk: AAD byte-encoding drift between TS and Rust produces no diagnostic, only "unseal failed" — the KAT fixture must be the first deliverable before any consumer phase begins.
- **Three pitfalls cause silent revocation bypass** with no runtime error: CRIT-1 (missing `fileKey'` mint in `rotateOne`), M1 generation high-water not persisted (evaporates on reload), and republisher still incrementing sequence (re-signs revoked CIDs at forward sequence, dominating the rotation publish).
- **HIGH-4 add-during-rotation is a silent data loss bug**, not a security bug: the 409-retry path re-sealing from stale in-memory `children[]` silently drops concurrent uploads with no error to either client.
- **The winfsp nesting trap and two-caller `spawn_file_meta_reencrypt`** are invisible on macOS; both require explicit Windows CI gates as phase completion criteria, not post-merge checks.
- **The `folder_ipns.public_key` null-row pattern** already caused two Phase-60 regressions and will recur in every FUSE and resolve path that doesn't use `publicKeyFromIpnsName` — dropping the column in the DB migration is the only reliable fix.

### Files Created

| File | Purpose |
| ---- | ------- |
| `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/research/PITFALLS.md` | 20 pitfalls with warning signs, prevention strategy, and owning phase |

### Confidence Assessment

| Area | Level | Reason |
| ---- | ----- | ------ |
| Cryptographic pitfalls | HIGH | Sourced directly from design findings CRIT-1/M1/HIGH-3/HIGH-4 + ADRs; KAT requirement is explicit in design |
| Rotation-engine pitfalls | HIGH | Design Sections 4.2–4.7 are detailed; HIGH-4/double-bump/crash-resume all fully specified |
| Revocation soundness | HIGH | ADR 0002 + design MED-5 + executeLazyRotation code references are precise |
| Write-plane/TEE pitfalls | HIGH | Session 2 grilling decisions 14–20 fully documented; `republish.ts:79` line reference confirmed |
| CipherBox-specific execution traps | HIGH | Drawn directly from project memory (Phase-60 regressions, winfsp trap, barrel coverage, SDK E2E gating) |

### Roadmap Implications

Phase ordering must be strict:

1. Crypto primitive phase first (`packages/crypto` KAT fixture committed before any consumer)
2. `packages/core` Node schema second (keystone — nothing else typechecks)
3. Rotation engine (`packages/sdk-core`) — must include CRIT-1, HIGH-3, HIGH-4 tests as success criteria
4. API DB cutover (atomic CAS, tombstone schema, generation gate, column drop) — before any E2E
5. TEE worker rewrite — before rotation E2E testing (sequence increment bug poisons rotation)
6. Web + FUSE phases — each needs SDK E2E and Windows CI as explicit completion gates, not optional

The three "silent revocation bypass" pitfalls (CRIT-1, M1 generation persistence, republisher sequence increment) must each be mandatory test-gated before the rotation engine phase can close. The rotation phase's success criteria should explicitly name design test items 1–6 as required.

## RESEARCH COMPLETE

**Project:** CipherBox v2.0 Metadata and Sharing Refactor (node/v3 integration mapping)
**Mode:** Architecture integration (brownfield)
**Confidence:** HIGH — all cited symbols verified against live code; no gaps in source material

### Key Findings

- All 18 design-cited symbol references verified. One minor line number discrepancy: `decryptFileMetadata` is at approximately line 231 in `packages/core/src/file/metadata.ts`, not 232 as cited — not material.
- The TEE republisher increment bug (`+ 1n` at `apps/tee-worker/src/routes/republish.ts:79`) is confirmed. The `unenrollIpns` tombstone gap is confirmed (`republish.service.ts:257` only deletes the schedule row).
- The `folder_ipns.public_key` column is confirmed nullable (`Buffer | null` at entity line 63); two Phase-60 regressions traced to it; safe to drop.
- The `encryptedChildKeys` JSONB fan-out on `share_invites` is confirmed and must be deleted.
- `originalFolderKeyEncrypted` re-encrypt-on-restore path is confirmed at `packages/sdk/src/bin/index.ts:688`; becomes dead code under node/v3 restore-as-pure-relink.
- The `parseCachedRecord`-null fall-through in `ipns.service.ts:504` is case-dependent (shared-folder expected-null vs signedRecord/CID mismatch corruption) — the fix is a two-branch case-split, not a single fail-closed.
- Cross-language AAD byte encoding failure is silent (returns `DecryptionError` with no source attribution) — the KAT must be phase 1's merge gate.

### Files Created

| File | Purpose |
| ---- | ------- |
| `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/research/ARCHITECTURE.md` | Layer-by-layer integration map, build order with dependency rationale, new/modified/deleted component lists, cross-cutting concerns, drift report |

### Confidence Assessment

| Area | Level | Reason |
| ---- | ----- | ------ |
| Build order | HIGH | Dependencies verified against actual imports; keystone (packages/core) confirmed by `packages/sdk-core` imports |
| New vs modified vs deleted | HIGH | All symbols grep-confirmed in live code |
| Cross-language parity surface | HIGH | Verified no AAD in current `seal.ts`; no generation field in `VerifiedResolve` struct |
| Phase 8 (FUSE) complexity | MEDIUM | Grant-root scope computation is net-new; Windows path CI-only; depth of change in `inode.rs` requires careful unwrap-replacement |

### Roadmap Implications

The 8-phase build order from the design (§7.2) is sound and reproduced with dependency justification. The roadmap planner should treat Phase 2 (packages/core keystone) as the critical-path bottleneck — nothing typechecks until it lands and `dist/` is rebuilt. Phase 5 (api) should be planned with TypeORM FK constraint analysis (all tables referencing `folder_ipns` must be migrated atomically). Phase 8 (FUSE) should budget a CI round-trip for Windows. The `pnpm api:generate` / `check-api-client.sh` gate fires on Phase 5 and must be satisfied before merge.

### Open Questions

- Phase 9.2 open question 3 from the design (write-recipient deletes owner-sub-shared node): authority model for the split-principal case is unresolved; the implementing phase needs a decision.
- Rotation host policy (design 9.2 open question 2): pure-web user without desktop pays a long multi-session rotation for large shared subtrees. UX decision needed in the web phase.

---

### Executive Summary

CipherBox v2.0 replaces a database-driven per-item key fan-out model (`share_keys` table, `O(items × recipients)` rows) with metadata-driven read key-chaining, and closes two confirmed revocation gaps: lazy/unsound read-revocation and un-rotatable write delegation. Scope is locked to Tier 1 (unified `Node` schema, read key-chain navigation, resumable read-rotation engine) and Tier 2 (write-revocation via full Ed25519 rotation — ADR 0001, and the resolve/republish/TEE contract rewrite). Tier 3 (capability-layer TTL/op-caps) is explicitly out of scope. The design is implementation-ready — three adversarial reviews, two grilling sessions, and two ratified ADRs; no design decisions remain open except the three §9.2 questions flagged below.

The build order is strictly dependency-constrained across 8 layers: crypto primitive → core keystone → sdk-core rotation engine → sdk write/bin/invite → api schema+publish-gate → tee-worker contract → web → fuse/winfsp. The AAD-bound seal primitive must land first because a byte-encoding mismatch between TS and Rust is a silent total decryption failure — no error, just broken unseals across every node. `packages/core` is the keystone: nothing below it typechecks until `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` exist and `dist/` is rebuilt.

The three most dangerous silent failure modes are: (1) CRIT-1 — rotating a file node without minting a new `fileKey'` leaves the revoked reader able to decrypt new content; (2) the M1 generation downgrade defense stored in memory only (evaporates on restart); (3) the TEE republisher continuing to increment `sequenceNumber` during rotation, making the stale pre-rotation CID dominate at a higher sequence. All three must be test-gated (§7.3 tests 2, 5, 12) before their phases close.

### Roadmap Implications

Suggested phases: 8

1. **Crypto Primitive** — `sealAesGcmAad`/`buildNodeAad` + TS↔Rust KAT fixture (all 4 role bytes); gate: tests 6+7
2. **Core Keystone** — unified `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` codecs; gate: all consumers typecheck after `dist/` rebuild
3. **sdk-core Rotation Engine** — `rotateReadFromNode`/`rotateOne`/`verifySubtreeClean` in named files (not barrels); gate: tests 1–5, 9; SDK E2E pass
4. **sdk Write/Bin/Invite** — structured write-body, delete fan-out, bin re-link, invite claim; gate: tests 10, 11
5. **API Schema + Publish Gate** — delete `share_keys`; rename `folder_ipns` → `ipns_records`; drop `public_key`; atomic CAS; tombstone; `api:generate`; gate: tests 13, 15, 16, 20
6. **TEE Worker Contract** — replace `+ 1n` with EOL-only extension; internal epoch derivation; name↔key binding; gate: tests 12, 17, 18, 19
7. **Web** — replace `executeLazyRotation`; durable IndexedDB high-water; reconcile-before-rotate; gate: test 5 (durable), test 13; `*.test.ts` naming enforced
8. **FUSE + WinFsp** — symmetric unwrap; delete `spawn_file_meta_reencrypt` from both callers; grant-root awareness; durable FUSE high-water; `Node` Rust enum; gate: test 21 (`Cargo Check & Test (Windows)` mandatory); desktop E2E explicit dispatch

### Research Flags

Needs sub-phase research: Phase 5 (TypeORM FK constraint map for `folder_ipns` rename — three FK references must be inspected against staging DB schema before writing migration); Phase 8 (grant-root scope computation algorithm is net-new and under-specified).

Standard patterns (skip research): Phase 1 (additive AES primitive), Phase 2 (schema fully specified), Phase 6 (surgical TEE rewrite, fully specified in design §6.2–6.7).

### Open Questions for Owning Phases

- **§9.2 Q1 — Co-writer offline handling** → Phase 7 (web)
- **§9.2 Q2 — Rotation host for pure-web users** → Phase 3 (sdk-core, document in plan)
- **§9.2 Q3 — Write-recipient-vs-owner sub-share authority** → Phase 7 (web) and Phase 8 (FUSE delete path)

### Confidence

Overall: HIGH. All cited symbols verified against live codebase (zero drift). Pitfalls sourced from design, ADRs, and project memory of prior regression patterns. Stack/Features not researched by design — scope locked from ratified design.

Gaps: Phase 5 FK map; Phase 8 grant-root algorithm; §9.2 open questions must be decided by owning phases.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md",
  "content": "# Requirements: CipherBox v2.0 Metadata and Sharing Refactor\n\n**Defined:** 2026-06-27\n**Core Value:** Zero-knowledge privacy — files encrypted client-side; the server is cryptographically unable to access user data.\n**Source of truth:** `.planning/design/2026-06-26-sharing-read-keychaining-design.md` + ADR 0001 (write-revocation = full Ed25519 rotation) + ADR 0002 (read-revoke protects future content only) + `CONTEXT.md` glossary.\n**Scope:** Tier 1 (read chain + resumable rotation) + Tier 2 (write-revocation + resolve/republish/TEE contract). Tier 3 out. Greenfield — `node/v3` is the sole codec, no migration.\n\n## v1 Requirements (this milestone)\n\nRequirements for v2.0. Each maps to exactly one roadmap phase. Categories: CRYPTO, NODE, READ, ROT, WRITE, TEE, DATA, TEST.\n\n### CRYPTO — AAD-bound seal primitive\n\n- [ ] **CRYPTO-01**: `packages/crypto` exposes `sealAesGcmAad`/`unsealAesGcmAad` + a canonical `buildNodeAad(domain‖nodeId‖kind‖generation‖role)` builder, each seal minting a fresh random IV\n- [ ] **CRYPTO-02**: A byte-identical Rust twin lives in `cipherbox_crypto`, with a committed cross-language KAT (frozen byte encoding; `kind` 0x01/0x02/0x03, raw 16-byte uuid, 4-byte BE generation, role ∈ {0x01 body, 0x02 child-readkey, 0x03 content, 0x04 child-writekey}) asserted by both TS and Rust\n- [ ] **CRYPTO-03**: A sealed blob replayed under a different `childId`/`role`/`generation` fails to unseal (AAD transplant resistance)\n\n### NODE — unified metadata model and codecs\n\n- [ ] **NODE-01**: A single `Node` model (folder/file/root via `kind`) with two independently sealed bodies — read-body under `readKey`, write-body under a separate `writeKey` — replaces `FolderMetadata`/`FileMetadata`/`FilePointer`/`FolderEntry`\n- [ ] **NODE-02**: A file node's `content` (incl. `content.fileKey`, and each `VersionEntry`'s inline `fileKey` + mandatory `encryptionMode` GCM/CTR) self-seals under the file node's own `readKey`\n- [ ] **NODE-03**: `SealedChildRef` is the read-only chain link (`name`, `ipnsName`, `generation` mirror, `versionFloor`, `readKeySealed`); the write link lives in the parent write-body, never in `SealedChildRef`\n- [ ] **NODE-04**: The published object is a plaintext envelope (`kind`/`id`/`generation`/`aeadVersion` + `readSealed`/`writeSealed`) with `generation` folded into AAD and tamper-evident\n- [ ] **NODE-05**: In Rust crates, `Node` is a real enum (`Folder { children } / File { content } / Root { children }`), not an `Option`-bag — impossible states unrepresentable\n- [ ] **NODE-06**: The vault recovery blob carries two keys — `ECIES(rootReadKey)` + `ECIES(rootWriteKey)` — re-designed (not migrated) for the root node's read + write bodies\n\n### READ — read key-chaining navigation and sharing\n\n- [ ] **READ-01**: A user can issue a read grant with one ECIES wrap of the share-root `readKey` + one `shares` row (0 node touches, 0 republishes); granting a single file is identical to granting a deep folder\n- [ ] **READ-02**: A grantee can navigate to a depth-`d` child via one ECIES unwrap then `O(depth)` symmetric AES, recovering content key/CID/mode at a file node; the read path distinguishes \"soft behind, retry\" from \"hard revoked\"\n- [ ] **READ-03**: Adding an item seals the child `readKey` under the parent `readKey` with no per-recipient fan-out; `reWrapForRecipients`/`addShareKeys` are deleted\n- [ ] **READ-04**: A move within a grantee's scope is link rewrites only (no re-encrypt), computing exact per-grant scope so benign within-scope moves do not over-rotate\n- [ ] **READ-05**: An invite wraps the single share-root `readKey` to an ephemeral key (private half in the URL fragment); claim re-wraps it to the claimer's key and stores a standard grant; the `encryptedChildKeys[]` fan-out is deleted\n\n### ROT — resumable read-rotation and revocation soundness\n\n- [ ] **ROT-01**: `rotateReadFromNode` is a resumable, per-node-commit, idempotent walk backing read-revoke and every scope-exit mutation; published IPNS records are the source of truth (job record advisory)\n- [ ] **ROT-02**: Rotation fires iff a node leaves a grantee's reachable scope; a node with no covering grant is a pure relink (zero rotations) — enforced as a hard test across delete/move/rename\n- [ ] **ROT-03**: (CRIT-1) Rotating a file node mints a new `fileKey` (lazy `contentRekeyPending`); a holder of the old `readKey`/`fileKey` cannot decrypt the next published version\n- [ ] **ROT-04**: (HIGH-3) Rotation re-mints `readDescriptorRef` for every non-revoked grant whose `rootNodeId` is in the rotated set — no orphaned inner grant\n- [ ] **ROT-05**: (HIGH-4) On a CAS-409 the walk re-fetches and re-merges `SealedChildRef`s rather than re-sealing from a stale child list — a concurrent add is never silently dropped\n- [ ] **ROT-06**: A crash mid-walk is recoverable — `verifySubtreeClean` rebuilds the frontier, re-run converges, no incorrect double-bump, and the revoked recipient is cut from the root after the root step\n- [ ] **ROT-07**: (M1) A durable client-side `{nodeId → highestGeneration}` high-water (survives restart, seeded from the grant `rootGeneration`) fails closed on generation regression\n\n### WRITE — write-revocation (Tier 2, ADR 0001)\n\n- [ ] **WRITE-01**: The write-body holds the node's Ed25519 signing material sealed under a separate `writeKey` as a structured recursive write chain (parent seals child `writeKey`, role `0x04`); a read-only holder can never reach signing material\n- [ ] **WRITE-02**: Write-revocation performs (c) full Ed25519 rotation — new keypair + k51 name per node, cascading parent re-points to the share root, re-pointing co-grants and owner devices\n- [ ] **WRITE-03**: Surviving co-writers receive the rotated Ed25519 key re-wrapped into their `writeDescriptorRef`; an offline co-writer cannot write until re-fetch (explicit)\n- [ ] **WRITE-04**: A rotated-out IPNS name is tombstoned (row kept) — the publish gate rejects all writes to it including the EOL-only renewal, resolve returns a tombstone/410, and the name is removed from the TEE republish batch\n\n### TEE — resolve, republish, and the TEE signing contract (Tier 2)\n\n- [ ] **TEE-01**: The TEE is a record-lease-renewer — it receives the marshaled `signedRecord`, verifies its signature, and re-emits the same CID and same sequence with only a later EOL; it cannot originate or repoint a CID\n- [ ] **TEE-02**: Republish never increments the sequence (the `+ 1n` republisher path is unified to no-increment); sequence-increment policy lives in the relay\n- [ ] **TEE-03**: The canonical `ipns_records` row is the sole source of the TEE's signing inputs; `ipns_republish_schedule`'s duplicated `latestCid`/`sequenceNumber`/`encryptedIpnsKey`/`keyEpoch` columns are collapsed\n- [ ] **TEE-04**: Publish is an atomic compare-and-set (`UPDATE … WHERE ipnsName = :n AND sequenceNumber = :expected`; 0 rows ⇒ 409); the EOL-only renewal is guarded identically so it can never regress `latestCid`/`sequenceNumber`\n- [ ] **TEE-05**: Resolve anti-rollback uses `generation` as the authority plus a durable per-node seq high-water and `versionFloor`; DB is canonical with a case-split fail-closed fall-through (expected-null shared-folder rows apply the seq floor; signedRecord-CID ≠ latestCid fails closed)\n- [ ] **TEE-06**: Enclave bindings are hardened — internal epoch self-derivation (never the relay's scalars), name↔key binding asserted before emit, and migration durability via a client recovery path\n- [ ] **TEE-07**: The publish gate enforces forward-only `generation` per node server-side (defence-in-depth, mirroring the sequence anti-rollback)\n\n### DATA — schema/DB cutover and bin\n\n- [ ] **DATA-01**: The `share_keys` table and entity are deleted outright (no dual-codec, no `version`-discriminator bridge)\n- [ ] **DATA-02**: `shares` is slimmed to one grant row per recipient carrying `readDescriptorRef`/`writeDescriptorRef` (legacy `readKeyEcies`/`ShareGrant` retired)\n- [ ] **DATA-03**: `folder_ipns` is renamed to `ipns_records` (entity `IpnsRecord`) and `folder_ipns.public_key` is dropped — the Ed25519 pubkey is always recovered from the k51 name via `publicKeyFromIpnsName`\n- [ ] **DATA-04**: A `BinEntry` is a `readKey`-sealed re-link; restore is a pure re-link (the `originalFolderKeyEncrypted` re-encrypt-on-restore path is deleted), a private delete is unlink + `BinEntry` (no rotation), and a shared delete rotates the departing subtree + revokes the grant rows\n\n### TEST — cross-cutting verification infrastructure\n\n- [ ] **TEST-01**: A rotation crash-safety/resume suite (the must-exist-before-merge suite) extends `tests/sdk-e2e` — the only real client→API IPNS publish/resolve round-trip — with abort-and-resume cases\n- [ ] **TEST-02**: The TS↔Rust AAD KAT is a single committed fixture asserted by both `packages/crypto/__tests__` and a Rust `#[test]` (a byte mismatch is silent total decryption failure)\n- [ ] **TEST-03**: The winfsp read-path is validated via `Cargo Check & Test (Windows)` (authoritative) and the dispatch-gated desktop E2E is triggered explicitly\n\n## Future Requirements (deferred)\n\n### Capability layer (Tier 3)\n\n- **CAP-01**: Write-plane time-boxing / op-count caps (`ttl`/`opCap`/`capabilityId` on the grant row) — only meaningful on the write path, only if a mediated mechanism is ever chosen; read-side TTL is cryptographically unenforceable. Do NOT add to `Node`/`SealedChildRef`.\n- **CAP-02**: Per-file \"re-encrypt now\" + `O(versions)` \"purge history\" for high-sensitivity content rotation\n- **CAP-03**: Lazy rotation *walk* (rotate-on-next-write across a subtree) — the `rotateOne` primitive is amortizable later if the eager cost proves painful\n\n### Infra\n\n- **INFRA-01**: SEED-001 Phala TEE on-demand cost cycling (stop/start the CVM around the republish window)\n\n## Out of Scope\n\nExplicitly excluded; documented to prevent scope creep.\n\n| Feature | Reason |\n| --- | --- |\n| Data migration / dual-codec bridge | Greenfield — no prod data, staging wiped; `node/v3` is the sole codec |\n| Mediated write signing (`POST /ipns/sign`, approach a/d) | Runner-up only; (c) full Ed25519 rotation is ratified (ADR 0001); turns the untrusted relay into a signing oracle |\n| Read-side TTL / op-caps | Cryptographically unenforceable — once a reader holds key + CID, IPFS serves it forever |\n| Retroactive content protection | Read-revoke protects future content/navigation only; already-distributed CIDs + prior versions stay readable (ADR 0002) |\n| Lazy rotation walk | Eager walk is the committed model this milestone |\n| Network-first resolve repoint | Stays a post-v2.0 v2 move; near-term DB-canonical with generation + seq-floor authority |\n| SEED-001 TEE cost cycling | Separable infra-cost optimization; deferred to a future infra milestone |\n| Encrypted Productivity Suite | Deferred to a post-v2.0 milestone |\n\n## Traceability\n\nWhich phases cover which requirements. Populated during roadmap creation.\n\n| Requirement | Phase | Status |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| (populated by roadmapper) | — | Pending |\n\n**Coverage:**\n\n- v1 requirements: 36 total\n- Mapped to phases: 0 (pending roadmap)\n- Unmapped: 36 ⚠️\n\n---\n\n_Requirements defined: 2026-06-27_\n_Last updated: 2026-06-27 after initial definition_\n"
}

### Milestone v2.0 Requirements

**CRYPTO** (3) — AAD-bound seal primitive + TS↔Rust KAT + transplant resistance
**NODE** (6) — unified `Node` (two sealed bodies), content self-seal, `SealedChildRef`, envelope, Rust enum, two-key recovery blob
**READ** (5) — grant issue, depth-`d` navigate, fan-out-free add, in-scope move, invites
**ROT** (7) — `rotateReadFromNode`, scope-exit rule, CRIT-1 fileKey mint, HIGH-3 multi-rooted re-mint, HIGH-4 add-during-rotation, crash-resume, M1 durable generation high-water
**WRITE** (4) — write-body chain, (c) full Ed25519 rotation, co-writer re-key, tombstone
**TEE** (7) — lease-renewer, no seq-increment, single canonical row, atomic CAS, resolve anti-rollback, enclave bindings, server generation gate
**DATA** (4) — delete `share_keys`, slim `shares`, rename `folder_ipns`→`ipns_records` + drop `public_key`, bin re-link
**TEST** (3) — crash-safety/resume sdk-e2e suite, TS↔Rust AAD KAT, winfsp/desktop CI gates

Tier 3 (capability layer / read-side TTL) + SEED-001 deferred to Future Requirements. Full file: `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md`.

75	### Phase 61: AAD-Bound Seal Primitive and Cross-Language KAT
76	
77	**Goal**: The canonical AES-GCM+AAD seal primitive and its frozen byte encoding exist in both TypeScript and Rust with a committed known-answer test proving byte-identical output.
78	
79	**Depends on**: Phase 60 (v1.1 complete)
80	
81	**Requirements**: CRYPTO-01, CRYPTO-02, CRYPTO-03, TEST-02
82	
83	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
84	
85	1. `sealAesGcmAad`/`unsealAesGcmAad`/`buildNodeAad` are exported from `packages/crypto` with each seal minting a fresh random IV
86	2. A byte-identical Rust twin exists in `crates/crypto` with the same AAD encoding (domain separator, raw UUID bytes, 4-byte BE generation, role bytes 0x01–0x04)
87	3. The cross-language KAT fixture — a single hardcoded vector covering all four role bytes — is asserted by both `packages/crypto/__tests__/build-node-aad.test.ts` AND a Rust `#[test]` in `crates/crypto/tests/cross_language.rs`; both pass in CI
88	4. A sealed blob replayed under a different `childId`, `role`, or `generation` fails to unseal (AAD transplant resistance test passes)
89	
90	**Plans**: TBD
91	
92	---
93	
94	### Phase 62: Unified Node Codec (Core Keystone)
95	
96	**Goal**: The unified `Node`/`SealedChildRef`/`PublishedNode` types and codecs exist in `packages/core`, replacing all `FolderMetadata`/`FileMetadata`/`FilePointer`/`FolderEntry` types; all downstream packages typecheck after `dist/` rebuild.
97	
98	**Depends on**: Phase 61
99	
100	**Requirements**: NODE-01, NODE-02, NODE-03, NODE-04, NODE-05, NODE-06
101	
102	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
103	
104	1. A single `Node` discriminated by `kind` (folder/file/root) carries two independently sealed bodies — `readSealed` under `readKey` and `writeSealed` under `writeKey` — and the published envelope exposes `generation` plaintext as the AAD epoch and anti-rollback witness
105	2. A file node's `content` (including `content.fileKey` and each `VersionEntry`'s inline `fileKey` + mandatory `encryptionMode`) self-seals under the file node's own `readKey`, not the parent's key
106	3. `SealedChildRef` contains name, `ipnsName`, `generation` mirror, `versionFloor`, and `readKeySealed` only — the write link is in the parent write-body exclusively
107	4. Vault recovery blob carries `ECIES(rootReadKey)` + `ECIES(rootWriteKey)` (two keys, one blob); old `encryptedRootFolderKey` field is removed
108	5. `packages/sdk-core`, `packages/sdk`, and `apps/web` typecheck cleanly after `packages/core` `dist/` is rebuilt — zero references to retired `FolderMetadata`/`FileMetadata`/`FilePointer`/`FolderEntry`
109	6. `METADATA_SCHEMAS.md` is updated to document the `generation`-as-convergence-witness invariant and the `fileKey`-inside-sealed-read-body semantic change
110	
111	**Plans**: TBD

115	### Phase 63: Read-Chain Navigation and Rotation Core
116	
117	**Goal**: The read key-chain navigation and rotation walk exist in `packages/sdk-core` as named implementation files; read grants require one ECIES unwrap then O(depth) symmetric AES; the scope-exit predicate gates every delete/move/rename.
118	
119	**Depends on**: Phase 62
120	
121	**Requirements**: READ-01, READ-02, READ-03, READ-04, READ-05, ROT-01, ROT-02
122	
123	**Open question (Q2)**: Document whether a large eager rotation in a browser-only (no desktop) session is acceptable — the rotation host question for pure-web users. Decision captured in the phase context file.
124	
125	**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):
126	
127	1. A read grant is issued by ECIES-wrapping the share-root `readKey` into one `shares` row (`readDescriptorRef`) with zero node touches and zero republishes; granting a single file is structurally identical to granting a deep folder
128	2. A grantee navigates to a depth-`d` child via one ECIES unwrap then `d` symmetric `unsealAesGcmAad` calls, recovering the content key and CID at a file node; the read path distinguishes "soft behind, retry" from "hard revoked" without ambiguity
129	3. Adding an item seals the child `readKey` under the parent `readKey` with no per-recipient fan-out; `reWrapForRecipients` and `addShareKeys` are deleted from the codebase
130	4. A move within a grantee's scope produces link rewrites only (zero re-encryption); the scope-exit predicate `hasCoveringGrant` is present and gates every delete/move/rename — a private delete with no active grants triggers zero `rotateReadFromNode` invocations and zero IPNS publishes beyond the parent relink (test verifies zero publish calls)
131	5. `rotateReadFromNode` is implemented in a named file (`src/rotation/engine.ts` or equivalent, not `index.ts` barrel) so vitest coverage counts it; `rotateOne` commits per-node atomically via CAS before advancing the walk frontier
132	
133	**Plans**: TBD
134
